# Release Plan — `install-resolves-identically`

**Milestone:** `install-resolves-identically` · milestone #338 · Stage-4 sub-task **#7684** = the approved plan (parts 1–2) and every decision record of this release · **#7750–#7764** = the eight Stage-5 designs · **#7859–#7866** = their eight adversarial reviews · **#7766** = the Stage-6 Engineering sub-task whose spoke authored this file (Commit 0), ahead of #6896's slice.

**Version identity:** **versioned** — bump-class **`minor`**, provisional display **`v4.73`**. The Stage-4 provisional `v4.69` was claimed by `egress-hook-batch` (PR #7638, tag `v4.69` on its merge commit), so the Collective Review re-determined the display as `v4.70`. The sibling `verifier-grades-what-plans-declare` then bound `v4.70` at its own Stage-12 claim (PR #7839, tag `v4.70` on its merge commit), so the Stage 7 batched fix cycle re-determined the display as `v4.71`, the next free slot (row ENG-BATCH-VER). The sibling `work-nature-and-axis-model` then bound `v4.71` at its own Stage-12 claim (PR #8004, tag `v4.71` on its merge commit), so the second Stage 7 sync re-determined the display as `v4.72`, the next free slot (row ENG-SYNC2-VER). The sibling `closeout-verification-rows-consistent` then bound `v4.72` at its own Stage-12 claim (PR #7895, tag `v4.72` on its merge commit), so the third sync re-determined the display as `v4.73`, the next free slot (row ENG-SYNC3-VER). Each is recorded as a determination (not a click-gate). The concrete `vX.Y` binds only at the Stage-12 atomic claim per ADR-092, so the plan file and the branch stay slug-primary while in flight and the Header `**Version**` cell carries the unresolved stamp placeholder. Other open releases display a provisional slot of their own choosing — at the third sync both open siblings still show `v4.70`, a bound slot (R6). The Stage-12 atomic claim serializes them; the loser re-versions forward. The Commit-0 re-verify ran in full, both halves — see § Commit-0 Version Re-Verify Record, which also records the batched cycle's, the second sync's and the third sync's re-determinations.

**Topology:** D-C **SINGLE** — one release branch (`release/install-resolves-identically`), one PR opened in draft at Commit 0 so CI runs while the slices land, one merge, base `main`. This plan lands as **Engineering Commit 0**; #6896's slice runs next on the same branch, then the other seven in serial order.

**Concurrency posture:** **P0 fully-serial**, one spoke in flight at a time (W_max 1, the operator's pacing decision recorded as DEV-15). Every non-serial posture prohibits force-push on the shared release branch; P0 is in force.

**Release class:** `cross-cutting`, ratified at the Stage-4 gate. Stage 9 review depth **Deep**. See § Release Class declaration.

> **Provenance.** This file transcribes the approved Stage-4 Release Planning output on #7684 (comments 5826287871 and 5826288647) and every decision record on #7684: the Stage-4 gate Decision Recorded (5826471133), the deviation-log addenda (5826554941, 5829665231, 5839221431), the Stage-5 launch-shape and quota-gate records (5826801615, 5829477009), the Collective Review evidence record (5840282367, 5840282806, 5840283143) and its Decision Recorded (5841113925), the Stage-6 entry record (5841775627) and the quota-to-cap Decision Recorded (5841863468). It is reconciled to the eight Stage-5 designs and their eight adversarial reviews. **Where a source's wording is ambiguous, the Decision Recorded governs the design and the design governs the review;** each such resolution is listed in § Decision Record → Transcription interpretations. Where a later disposition superseded a Stage-4 value, the transcribed section carries the ratified value and § Deviation Log records the delta with its authority. Every thread comment consumed was `OWNER`-authored (Comment-Ingestion Trust Boundary). Security-relevant detail from the designs and reviews is not carried here; an in-scope fix is stated only at the level its rendered decision states it.

## Commit-0 Version Re-Verify Record

Run in full at Engineering Commit 0, both halves, per `release/references/how-to/hub-spoke-bridge.md` Procedure 0 § Canonical location (steps 1–3, then 3b).

### Version half (steps 1–3, pre-write)

| Step | Action | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | `git fetch --tags origin`, then `git fetch origin main` | both exit 0; `origin/main` = `35dbf418`, the Collective Review assembly base (the Stage-4 pin was `8e0ee084`) |
| **2** | Recompute next-free for bump-class **`minor`** through the adapter itself: `release/tools/claim-version.sh --sha 35dbf41847df2c1deab792d2944e46ac6ddd26fd --bump minor --dry-run`, run with `CLAIM_REPO=<owner>/<repo>` so the claimed-set read uses the REST arm (the adapter's own `anchor()` + `claimed_set()`; no tag pushed) | **`v4.70`** — "would claim v4.70 (no tag pushed)"; equal to the planned display |
| **3** | HALT on collision: the planned version must be absent from every `claimed_set()` arm AND equal the recomputed next-free. The tag arm binds; published Releases and the RELEASE_LOG corroborate | **no collision; PROCEED** |

**Probe record for the step-3 zero** (per `core/disciplines/review-discipline-principles.md` § 8, elements PV-0..PV-7):

```
Probe:       git ls-remote --tags origin 'refs/tags/v4.70*'                     (tag arm — binds)
             the published-Releases list, REST, paginated → tag names v4.70*     (Releases arm)
             v4.70 followed by a non-digit or end of line, over the ledger read
             with git show origin/main:release/releases/RELEASE_LOG.md           (ledger arm)
Denominator: 214 v* origin tags (peeled refs excluded); 212 published Releases (paginated,
             so the read is not truncated); RELEASE_LOG at origin/main, 2618 lines
Control - sensitivity: the SAME three readers on the v4.69 slot — tag v4.69 present (the
             annotated tag peels to 40cec0c7, the egress-hook-batch merge); Releases 1;
             ledger 5 lines. Every arm resolves and returns non-zero, so a zero on the
             v4.70 slot is a real negative
Control - specificity: the ledger pattern over a planted pair of lines, one naming v4.700
             and one naming v4.70 → 1 (the v4.70 line only). Tag and Releases arms: NOT
             TRIGGERED — an exact slot tuple has no near-miss class beyond the glob, which
             is broader than the tuple, so its zero implies the tuple's zero
Extraction:  the full ls-remote output; the full paginated Releases list; the full ledger
             read from origin/main (never the worktree copy)
Result:      0 occupants of the (4,70) slot on every arm; v4.71 through v4.79 → 0 on the
             Releases arm
Verdict:     CLEAN — v4.70 is free and equals the recomputed next-free; no HALT
```

**Re-determined at the Stage 7 batched fix cycle (2026-10-02, Friday, UTC):** with `v4.70` bound by the sibling `verifier-grades-what-plans-declare`, step 2's dry-run, run at the branch head `4cfdbf44` with `CLAIM_REPO` set, printed `claim-version: dry-run — would claim v4.71 (no tag pushed)` — row ENG-BATCH-VER records it, and the number still binds only at the Stage-12 atomic claim.

**Re-determined at the second Stage 7 sync (2026-10-02, Friday, UTC):** with `v4.71` bound by the sibling `work-nature-and-axis-model` (the annotated tag peels to its merge commit `82ad090f`; no `v4.72` ref exists), step 2's dry-run, run at the branch head `2590f934` with `CLAIM_REPO` set, printed `claim-version: dry-run — would claim v4.72 (no tag pushed)` — row ENG-SYNC2-VER records it, and the number still binds only at the Stage-12 atomic claim.

**Re-determined at the third sync (2026-10-03, Saturday, UTC):** with `v4.72` bound by the sibling `closeout-verification-rows-consistent` (the annotated tag peels to its merge commit `ad5b996e`; no `v4.73` ref exists), step 2's dry-run, run at the branch head `02a349f4` with `CLAIM_REPO` set, printed `claim-version: dry-run — would claim v4.73 (no tag pushed)` — row ENG-SYNC3-VER records it, and the number still binds only at the Stage-12 atomic claim.

**In-flight state at Commit 0:** `git ls-remote --heads origin 'release/*'` → **4** heads (`controls-fail-loud`, `telemetry-is-computable`, `closeout-verification-rows-consistent`, `verifier-grades-what-plans-declare`); this release's head was absent before creation (0; control: `main` → 1). Open PRs → **4**, all draft, all OWNER-authored — the same four branches (§ In-Flight Release Roster).

**ADR numbering (Q3) — nothing is allocated at Commit 0.** The mainline anchor at `35dbf418` is 206: 206 ADR files across `core/ADRs/` and `release/ADRs/`, maximum 206, no duplicate and no gap (control: the two directory READMEs are excluded by the ADR-file pattern). At Commit 0 the `verifier-grades-what-plans-declare` draft branch (ms#405, PR #7839) holds four `release/ADRs/` numbers, 207 through 210 — the Collective Review measured three — and ms#395 plans two more. This release's three records are numbered literally at authorship, in serial order (#5265, then #5274, then #6440), and cited in prose by the ADR slug token (the double-brace `ADR:` form); Stage-12 A.5.7 reconciles if another release merges ADRs first.

### Manifest half (step 3b, post-write / pre-commit)

`release/tools/claim-version.sh --verify-stamp install-resolves-identically` — run after this file was written and before it was committed. Required exit **0**. Result recorded in § Verification Evidence.

This plan carries **exactly one** double-brace `RELEASE_VERSION` placeholder — the Header `**Version**` cell — and every other mention names the placeholder instead of reproducing it. The claim tool resolves the token by global substitution across the whole file, so a literal prose citation would be rewritten at Stage 12 along with the record site.

## Scope

**Eight members, one root-cause class** — C19, a scope predicate anchored on invocation context rather than on the governed subject. Zero native dependency edges (16 reads, with a live control); the edges below are derived from shared surfaces and composition.

| # | Issue | The defect this slice removes | Size (pts) | Design · review |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | #6896 | The hook-suite layout helper's documented invocation is refused by BLOCK-DESTRUCTIVE-022: the default sandbox is an unallowlisted temp path, and the documented runner line carries a variable | S (2) | #7750 · #7859 |
| 2 | #4993 | The standing install regression reports FAIL under its own sandboxed HOME, while its R-8 safety proof passes on an empty subject | M (4) | #7752 · #7860 |
| 3 | #4994 | An unattended (`--non-interactive`) install hangs when stdin is open: three human-answer prompts are unguarded | M (4) | #7754 · #7861 |
| 4 | #5265 | `[PMO_PLATFORM_ROOT]` resolves by self-location, so a refresh run from a worktree bakes that worktree's path into deployed surfaces | S (2) | #7756 · #7862 |
| 5 | #6168 | The composed script allowlist depends on which checkout ran the refresh, and the refresh does not say which root it bound | S (2) | #7758 · #7863 |
| 6 | #5274 | The skill-editor exemption list resolves to four paths across five consumers, and the update completeness gate covers the hook tier only | L (8) | #7760 · #7864 |
| 7 | #7497 | BLOCK-DESTRUCTIVE-019's worktree exemption keys on the session's working directory instead of the write target | M (4) | #7762 · #7865 |
| 8 | #6440 | Evicting an auto-memory entry — a Trash move — is structurally unexecutable: the deletion-containment hook refuses every target outside the workspace root | M (4) | #7764 · #7866 |

**Size:** 30 raw points → **effective 39** (`round_half_up(30 × 1.3)`, class `cross-cutting`), above the 15–25 band. The G3-15 Override is re-recorded at 39 (Q10), superseding the Stage-4 Override at 30 (DEV-1). The resizes: #6896 XS → S, #7497 S → M, #5274 M → L.

**Acceptance criteria.** 37 criteria across the eight cards: #6896 3 · #4993 5 · #4994 6 · #5265 4 · #6168 4 · #5274 5 · #7497 7 · #6440 3. #7497 carried 5 at Commit 0; the hub added AC-6 and AC-7 to the card at Stage 6 from § Proposed criteria, and slice 7 binds them. The criteria's single home is each issue body; § Verification Plan binds each one by ordinal and never restates it.

**Explicit non-scope.**
- #6198 (ms#402): a co-discharge candidate for #5274's end-to-end arm and layout materialization. It is not pulled in; AI-001 grades it at Stage 13.
- #5633 (ms#366): the enforcing-first hazard notice was posted at the Collective Review (Q11).
- F1, the other five vacuous row-5 live-install detectives, routed as a follow-up (Q7).
- The worktree-base drift at `core/rules/git-workflow.md:39`, routed out under the N-way R14 override (Q9).
- #6440's stated residual: eviction has no read-back.
- #5185's ARCHIVE destination (ms#352).

**Column 7, re-derived at the Stage 7 sync.** Each cell names the rows the selection map selects for the card's File Change Matrix paths, resolved on the map as merged with `main`: its glob grammar, git's pathspec with the `top` and `glob` magic words as the reference resolver, and precedence by the longest literal prefix. Rows 1 to 5 keep their numbers, and the no-match fallback is the map's last row. The cells for #6896, #4993, #4994, #5265 and #5274 changed; § Implementation Notes → Stage 7 sync cycle shows each cell before and after.

**Release-scoped stages:**
- **Stage 9 Plan Review:** Deep (class).
- **Stage 10 Dry Run: ACTIVE.** The deploy mechanism changes — the resolver behind every composed surface (#5265/#6168) and the exemption-list target (#5274). The recipe pins `PMO_PLATFORM_CONFIG_ROOT` and `HOME` to the sandbox (#7863 PR-2). It composes from the primary checkout and from a worktree and compares them with Arm 8's `managed_at`-normalized comparison over the manifest-resolved set — **never `diff -r`** (Q5) — and reads each refresh's report line by fixed string (`grep -F`, or a prefix test), expecting exactly one line per refresh and rendering both of its paths as placeholders when quoting it. It runs a sandbox install → update cycle with #5274's probes (the list at its resolved location; update exit 0/64; the list removed → 75; a lossless legacy copy retired by the reconcile phase; a legacy copy with an extra entry WARNed and kept).
- **Stage 11 Snapshot:** compressed (git, plus the installer's durable hook snapshot and backups).
- **Stage 12 Execute:** APPLY — merge, atomic claim, hook-bundle republish (Check 79), surface regeneration, the rules-mirror redeploy for `core/rules/skill-deployment.md` (Check 9). **Operator-observed row (Success Indicator clause 2, Q4):** after the operator's `./update.sh` from the primary checkout, a `--surfaces-only --force-regen` refresh run from a linked worktree at the merged commit leaves the deployed allowlist identical apart from `managed_at`, with every token row bound to the primary. The report line is read by fixed string (`grep -F`, or a prefix test), exactly one line is expected, and it is quoted with both of its paths rendered as placeholders: the root as `<primary-checkout>` and the template checkout as `<invoking-checkout>`.
- **Stage 13 Close:** APPLY (30-day outcome window). The eight cards transition to closed here; AI-001 grades #6198; #5274 AC-5 is recorded on #4449.

- **Repository-root rows.** `update.sh` (three rows) and `.gitignore` carry no directory segment, so the plan-driven executor's first-segment enum reads them as `fence-unrecognized-path` (FCM-COVERAGE SKIP, `fcm-rows-uninterpreted`). Each is an EDIT, so no ADD obligation is lost.
- **Counts.** 77 unconditional rows over 58 distinct paths: 5 ADDs (this plan, the new suite and three ADRs) and 53 edited paths. Eight CONDITIONAL rows are counted apart — the optional lib arm resolved false at slice 4 (row ND-6), and the seven of the resolved-false block. Across every row, 63 distinct paths are declared. The Stage 7 batched fix cycle adds one row and one edited path, `release/references/pipeline/stage-04-planning.md` in #5274's block (PA-007, row ENG-BATCH-PA007), and annotates three of #5265's rows for PA-008 with no count moving; before it the counts read 76 rows, 57 paths, 52 edited, eight conditional rows and 62 declared. Commit 0 declared 70 rows, 54 paths, 49 of them edited and 60 in all; slice 1's Tier-1 row (ENG-6896-ADJ) adds the one edited path; slice 2's PA-001 row adds a third row for `.github/workflows/install-tests.yml`, a path already declared, so the path counts are unchanged; slice 2's second pass (PA-003, row ENG-4993-PA003) adds `core/hooks/tests/block-fs-boundary.test.sh`, a newly declared edited path; slice 3's PA-004 row (ENG-4994-PA004) adds a fourth row for `.github/workflows/install-tests.yml`, so again only the row count moves; slice 6's Tier-1 row (ENG-5274-ADJ) adds a second row for `core/hooks/tests/setup-ci-layout.test.sh`, a path already declared as #6896's ADD, so only the row count moves again; slice 8's PA-003 scope row (ENG-6440-PA003) annotates #6440's existing row for `core/hooks/tests/block-rm-prefer-trash.test.sh`, and its ADR row takes its literal number, so no count moves; the Stage 7 sync promotes the ADR README's row, whose condition fired when the renumber tool wrote its Renumber-log lines (row ENG-SYNC-ADR), so one row and one edited path move from the conditional set to the unconditional one, and the three ADR rows take their new filenames with no count moving. The second Stage 7 sync renames #5265's ADR row once more (row ENG-SYNC2-ADR), and no count moves. The third sync renames #5274's and #6440's ADR rows (row ENG-SYNC3-ADR), and no count moves. Before the first sync the counts read 75 rows, 56 paths, 51 edited and nine conditional rows, one of them live. The allowlist's token population is **168 rows (171 lines)** at the pin (Q5).
- **New-executable companion obligation: does not fire.** `CONDITIONAL:RESOLVER-LIB-ADDED` resolved false — the resolver lands in `core/deploy/lib-instance-path.sh`, which the hook bundle already co-deploys. The one new shell file, `core/hooks/tests/setup-ci-layout.test.sh`, is a suite the runner discovers by its `*.test.sh` glob; no allowlist row changes (#7750).
- **ADR rows.** The three `ADR-NNN` placeholders take literal numbers when their slices author them (Q3); the `NNN` form is the matrix contract's glob for a not-yet-numbered record, never a filename. #5265's record, {{ADR:pmo-platform-root-resolves-from-install-record}}, took the number 207 at slice 4, claimed against the mainline anchor 206. #5274's record, {{ADR:exemption-list-resolves-at-the-instance-tier-through-one-resolver}}, took 208 at slice 6 against the same anchor, the next slot on this branch. #6440's record, {{ADR:memory-eviction-is-not-an-outside-workspace-deletion}}, took 209 at slice 8 against the same anchor, the third slot on this branch; no record is left unnumbered. At the Stage 7 sync the renumber tool moved the three above `main`'s anchor of 210, in authorship order — `207 → 211`, `208 → 212` and `209 → 213` — because `main` had merged four records numbered 207 to 210 (row ENG-SYNC-ADR). Each record's Status section carries the tool's provenance note, and the three rows above carry the new filenames. At the second Stage 7 sync `main` held 211, the record of the merged sibling `work-nature-and-axis-model`, so #5265's record duplicated it. The tool held #5274's record at 212 and #6440's at 213 — each already sat in the free range 212 to 214 the merged union needs, on a number the mainline does not hold — and moved #5265's record to the one open slot, 214 (row ENG-SYNC2-ADR); its Status section carries a second provenance note. The numbers therefore no longer follow authorship order: the tool's assignment is minimal, and moves only the claim that collides. At the third sync, run during Stage 8, `main` held 212 and 213, the two records the merged sibling `closeout-verification-rows-consistent` added under `release/ADRs/`, so #5274's and #6440's records duplicated them. The tool held #5265's record at 214 — inside the free range 214 to 216 the merged union needs, on a number the mainline does not hold — and moved the two colliding records to the two open slots, in the order it printed: #5274's to 215, then #6440's to 216 (row ENG-SYNC3-ADR). Each of the two records' Status sections carries a second provenance note. The numbers now run 214, 215 and 216 for #5265's, #5274's and #6440's records, which is authorship order again.

## Delivery Strategy

| Aspect | Decision |
|---|---|
| Implementation approach | Sequential, P0 fully-serial, along the eight-slice chain; one spoke in flight at a time |
| Commit strategy | Per slice, a RED commit (the arms), then a GREEN commit (the fix), with RED and GREEN read from the PR's check runs (Q1). The index is regenerated inside the fragment-editing commit. Commit messages carry the `release(install-resolves-identically):` prefix and name the card. This plan is Engineering Commit 0 |
| Review approach | A single PR for the whole release (D-C SINGLE), opened in draft at Commit 0. The body is parser-clean: close-family verbs next to an issue number appear only in the dedicated Issue References block; the eight cards transition to closed at Stage 13 |
| Deployment mechanism | Git merge + atomic version claim + hook-tier republish (`update.sh` → `setup-workspace.sh --refresh-hooks`, the co-deployed `lib-instance-path.sh` included) + composition-surface regeneration (`update.sh`; the HL-1 heal on the first post-release update) + the rules-mirror redeploy of `core/rules/skill-deployment.md` (`deploy.sh --deploy`). **No skill S-2 deploy and no package rebuild** — measured: `build-skill-packages.sh --skills-for-paths` over the 62 declared paths returns no skill (re-measured at slice 1 and at slice 2's second pass; 60 paths at Commit 0) |
| Stacked-base cleanup posture | N/A — single branch |

---

## Verification Plan

**Verification locus (Q1).** CI is the observer: RED and GREEN are read from the pull request's check runs on the slice commits. Any local run sets `PMO_REGRESSION_EMIT=0`. Every method that runs the regression under an overridden HOME — #4993's Step 0, AC-1, AC-3 (ii) and AC-5, CIAC-2, and #7497's INT-5 — is graded from the CI record, not from a spoke-run `HOME=` prefix; the user-base pin is computed with `/usr/bin/python3`. At Commit 0 no CI step produces that record (R18), so those rows read INDETERMINATE until it exists — never PASS. A hook-suite run by a spoke uses #6896's documented invocation from its own worktree root, the site Q2's scoped carve-out admits.

**Every method cell carries its command literally**, reproducible from the cell alone. A `bash …` cell is dispatchable but **not executed** by the plan-driven executor `release/tools/verify-release-plan.sh`, whose runnable-verb set is closed to read-only queries; such a row reads as ERROR (`unclassified-method`) there, and its guarantee lives in the suite's own CI-invoked run. At Commit 0 every verdict is a pre-fix reading by construction.

## Cross-Issue Acceptance Criteria

**Cross-Issue Acceptance Criteria** — graded at Stage 9 QC3.5 on the merged PR, as corrected by Q5. Each stays a cross-issue criterion because at least two of its cards edit or depend on the named surface.
- [ ] **CIAC-1 (#5265 × #6168 on `resolve_repo_root` and the composed allowlist):** A composition refresh run from the primary checkout, from a nested worktree and from an out-of-tree worktree, against one sandbox workspace, composes surface sets identical apart from their `managed_at` marker lines; every run rewrites every manifest-resolved target; and on each run every template token row is bound to the canonical primary root (the root-equality probe: 168 token rows, distinct worktree-glob roots equal to that root). *Method:* `grep -c -F "Arm 8: caller-independence" core/deploy/tests/test_refresh_surfaces.sh` at least 1 — a label-presence probe on the arm that asserts the predicate, Arm 8 as amended by #6168's A-3 (the named member matched by basename); the suite's own CI run grades the arm's behavior, and its control's, in the install-regression job's two install steps (Q1). *Graded at Stage 9 QC3.5 on the merged PR.* Control: Arm 8c, where one branch-only tool block changes the set by exactly that block, bound to the primary. Stage 10 repeats the comparison and never uses `diff -r`; the naive `/.claude/worktrees/` segment count is not a criterion (86 on a correct build).
- [ ] **CIAC-2 (#4993 × #4994 on `test_upgrade_config_durability.sh` and `run-install-regression.sh`):** The standing regression prints `VERDICT PASS` under the selection map's § 3 row-5 recipe — `HOME` a fresh `mktemp -d`, `PYTHONUSERBASE` pinned from `/usr/bin/python3` before the override — and under the real HOME, each verdict line's stamp naming its environment; and the durability suite carries at least one `--non-interactive` invocation with stdin open. *Method:* declared, verification deferred to the CI record (Q1) in `.github/workflows/install-tests.yml` of `bash core/deploy/tests/run-install-regression.sh` under each environment with `PMO_REGRESSION_EMIT=0` (the override leg is the install-regression job's row-5 step, PA-001), and to `core/deploy/tests/test_upgrade_config_durability.sh`, whose backslash-continued lines, joined, carry `"${SETUP}"` invocations with `--non-interactive` and without `0<&-` → at least 1 (the design's GREEN count is 3) · control: the same joined count of closed-stdin `--non-interactive` invocations → 10; all `"${SETUP}"` invocations → 14 at `8e0ee084`. *Graded at Stage 9 QC3.5 on the merged PR.*
- [ ] **CIAC-3 (#4994 × #5265 on `docs/scripts/setup-workspace.sh`):** The install-time root-recording step adds no unguarded prompt: every human-answer `read` in the installer sits behind the `NON_INTERACTIVE` guard (guarded equals total), a `read` fed by input redirection on its own line counting as a data read (Q6); and a stdin-open `--non-interactive` install from a linked worktree completes and records the canonical root. *Method:* declared, verification deferred to the independent oracle Q5 names (FM-4 of the #5265 review) — `bash core/deploy/tests/test_refresh_surfaces.sh` Arm 7 run under #4994's open-stdin harness shape (`run_open_stdin`), comparing the recorded `source_repo_path` with `pwd -P` of the fixture primary, never with the resolver's own output — and to the P-10 census in `bash core/deploy/tests/test_upgrade_config_durability.sh` → `5 5 -`, both graded from the install-regression job's two install steps (Q1) · control: the pre-fix installer reads `5 1 2440,2870,3543,4467`. *Graded at Stage 9 QC3.5 on the merged PR.*
- [ ] **CIAC-4 (#5274 × #6896 on the exemption list across layouts):** The path `block-skill-direct-edit.sh` resolves for its exemption list exists and carries the canary in a fresh sandbox install and in the `setup-ci-layout.sh` layout (its default site and an explicit `--sandbox`); step 3b writes it through #6896's recorded footprint, so the purge removes it on rebuild; and the deployed-shape arm E2E-6 — the hook and the writer run with `CLAUDE_WORKSPACE_ROOT` and `PMO_INSTANCE_PATH` unset — grants the exemption. *Method:* declared, verification deferred to `bash core/hooks/tests/block-skill-direct-edit.test.sh` (Test 3 and 3b, E2E-1..E2E-6) and `bash core/hooks/tests/setup-ci-layout.test.sh` (the footprint and purge arms) — each suite's total and FAIL lines read from the CI hook-tests log (Q1), which prints no line for a passing arm, and the arms from each suite's source at the graded SHA · control: the pre-fix CI layout lacks the file (the co-discharge candidate's finding), and E2E-6's mutation — the hook's root derivation one level short — turns it RED. *Graded at Stage 9 QC3.5 on the merged PR.*
- [ ] **CIAC-5 (#7497 × #6440 × #5274 on `core/rules/bypass-mode-readiness.md`):** The committed index equals the generator's output, and the shipped rows describe the shipped predicates: -019 target-keyed (#7497's cascade-sweep file set carries 0 superseded spellings) and the BLOCK-TRASH memory-store arm (#6440's new wording present in the fragment and the index, 0 at baseline). *Method:* declared, verification deferred to three readings over `core/rules/bypass-mode-readiness.md` and its fragments: `python3 core/deploy/tools/build-hook-registry.py --check` → exit 0, whose CI record is the pre-merge load-bearing check subset's index-freshness line (Check 38); `grep -c -i 'auto-memory' core/rules/bypass-mode-readiness/block-rm-prefer-trash.md` → at least 3, and the same count over `core/rules/bypass-mode-readiness.md` → at least 3; #7497's Cascade-Sweep command over its five non-test files → 0 · control: the sweep reads 13 on the pre-change files. *Graded at Stage 9 QC3.5 on the merged PR.*

## Release Class declaration

**`cross-cutting`** — ratified at the Stage-4 gate (DEV-5 wrote the milestone's rationale in template form).

- **Rationale:** eight fixes share root-cause class C19 (a scope predicate anchored on invocation context, not the governed subject) and compose over shared surfaces — the compose resolver, the install durability suite, the hook-suite runner and the generated hook registry — giving ≥3 in-bundle compositional edges (trigger c). Novel triggers also fire (Stage-5 design decisions), and cross-cutting dominates under multi-trigger resolution.
- **Differentiation posture:** Engagement **Tight** · Stage 9 review **Deep** · Stage 5 bias **ALL** · outcome window **30-day**.
- **Size bound:** `effective_pts: raw 30 × 1.3 = 39 — above band vs 15-25`, kept under the G3-15 Override (Q10).

## Release Outcome Statement

Transcribed from the milestone description as refreshed at the Collective Review (Q4).

**AFTER** — Install, deploy and guard decisions resolve from the subject they govern — the canonical repository, the declared surface path, the write target — rather than from where they were invoked. A refresh from any checkout, an unattended install with stdin open, and the regression suite under a sandboxed HOME each produce the same result as their canonical counterpart, and the platform's guards admit the paths their documented procedures use while still refusing everything else.

**BEFORE** — Several paths key on the caller's context instead: a refresh from a worktree stamps that worktree's path into deployed surfaces, one exemption list resolves to four paths across five consumers, an unattended install hangs when stdin is open, and the regression suite reports FAIL under its own sandbox while its safety proof passes on an empty subject. Guards decide on the session's working directory or the workspace root rather than the target, so documented invocations are refused.

**Actor(s):** operators running install and update from any checkout; Stage 6–8 spokes running the hook and install suites.

**Success Indicator:** On the merged tree, `run-install-regression.sh` prints `VERDICT PASS` under both `HOME=$(mktemp -d)` — read as the runtime-suite selection map's § 3 row-5 recipe, including its user-base pin — and the real HOME. At Stage 12, after the operator's `./update.sh` from the primary checkout, a `--surfaces-only --force-regen` refresh run from a linked worktree at the merged commit leaves the deployed allowlist identical apart from `managed_at`, with every token row bound to the primary.

## Rollback Strategy

| Issue | Rollback method | Complexity |
|---|---|---|
| #6896 | `git revert` of the slice commits, callers included. **Keep the new `.gitignore` lines** (or sweep the checkout-root layouts first): a revert that drops them, or a branch switch in a populated worktree onto a base cut before this release merges, leaves every populated checkout one `git add` away from committing token-resolved files that carry a home path (#7859 PR-3) | Low |
| #4993 | `git revert` of the slice | Low |
| #4994 | `git revert` of the slice; the flagged recovery exit returns to 0 | Low |
| #5265 + #6168 | Revert both slices together (the shared `test_refresh_surfaces.sh` arm block; the resolver and its report line), then regenerate surfaces from the primary checkout. A canonical `source_repo_path` and its provenance key are inert to the prior resolver | Medium |
| #5274 | Revert the slice; the gate widening reverts with it. A retired legacy copy is restored from its backup; an operator entry added at the canonical path after merge must be re-added at the hook-tier copy | Medium |
| #7497 | Revert, then republish the hook. Reverting **re-opens** the worktree → primary Write/Edit path and reinstates the roadmaps refusal for primary-rooted sessions. Verify with the -019 arms | Medium (MODERATE) |
| #6440 | Revert, then republish the hook; item 8a reverts with it. A numbered, merged ADR is superseded, never deleted (the gap-free sequence). Evictions already made are recoverable from Trash | Medium (MODERATE) |

**Whole release:** a partial revert per slice (regenerate the index after any fragment revert); a full restore by `git revert -m 1 <merge>`, then a hook republish and a surface regeneration from the primary checkout; a forward fix is preferred for a well-understood defect. **A claimed version tag is retained and recorded, never deleted** — version tags are host-protected. After the claim, a merge revert stops once, on this plan's claim-stamp rename: keep the stamped plan, `git add` it and continue.

| **ENG-5265-ARM10** | [ADJUST] Tier 1 — a test defect in the merged Change 8's Arm 10, found by CI and repaired test-only in `core/deploy/tests/test_refresh_surfaces.sh`. The line "a plain refresh (no --force-regen) regenerates exactly the poisoned surface" FAILed on all four slice-4 commits, in both install steps, with one detail: the stamp helper found no `managed_at` marker on the poisoned allowlist's line 4. A composed file carries its markers in a fixed order — `managed_sha`, `installed_sha`, then `managed_at` on line 4 — and the stamp helper rewrites line 4 of every target. Arm 10 stamped after the poison, and the poison drops the `installed_sha` line, so the marker sat on line 3 and the line failed whatever the heal did. A second defect sat under the first: with no marker on its line 4, the poisoned surface reads as rewritten whether or not the refresh wrote it, so the line's "none rewritten" half rested on the exit code alone. The repair, in Arm 10 only: the stamp moves above the poison, and after the refresh the snapshot helper's check runs over the target set; it needs a `managed_at` marker on each target's line 4 and one `installed_sha` line, which the poisoned surface regains only through a composition write. The stamp helper, every other arm and every file under test are unchanged. Each half was then armed red — the refresh as a dry run (nothing rewritten), and forced (every surface rewritten) — and reverted to the repaired tree. The merged Change 8's § 7 carries the repaired text through an erratum posted with the fix cycle's output on #7772 | CI: the install-regression job's two install steps on `19bf94d0`, `a21716d0`, `53b17526` and `6e0dcc9f`; the Stage 6 authorization at the scope-lock, #7684 comment 5841113925, within which a defect a slice's own CI finds inside its own file set is repaired; `stage-06-engineering.md` § 5 B3 | **APPLIED** — FIX `380daacc`; armed red at `1048d259` and `8be3916d`, reverted at `ff5c0317`, whose tree equals `380daacc`'s; § Implementation Notes → Slice 4 |

| **ENG-6440-READINGS** | [ADJUST] Tier 1 — readings of the rendered design inside #6440's file set. (1) **Operand first.** The predicate judges the operand before it opens any settings file. A conjunction is order-free in result, so a command whose operand cannot be an entry never reads one; that covers every pre-existing arm of the suite, which keeps the caller's HOME. (2) **The operand is judged as the shell will read it.** It must be written only in characters the shell passes through unchanged, and a quoted operand is not tilde-expanded. Only its parent is resolved, and its leaf must be an existing regular file and not a symbolic link. The design resolved the whole operand; Trash moves the operand as written, so the path the hook classifies must be the path the command removes — a binding that holds while the operand's directories are unchanged between judgment and execution, the class every path-resolving rule in the registry carries (F-42). MEM-10 and MEM-18 carry the extra payloads that pin this, each passing only when every payload is refused. MEM-10's store holds each expansion form as a literal file name, so its character rule is graded on its own. (3) **CD-2's sink and shape.** The row goes to the hook's `block-log.jsonl`, the one candidate the hook-publish map already classes as operator state. It is compact JSON with `action: memory-evict-admit`, the command's digest and the working directory: never the command or the entry's path (ADR-205's floor), and no `rule` key. Rows are written once every operand has been judged, so a command refused as a whole leaves none. An admission whose row cannot be written is refused, so "every admission leaves a row" holds rather than being best-effort. (4) **FM-3's source.** Project and local settings are read under the project root Claude Code passes its hooks, else under the payload's working directory. They are read only to word the refusal, which fires only where the entry would be admitted had the store been declared in user scope. (5) **The Tier-A marker.** The design-artifact standard requires a declaration marker under the artifact's own heading. The paragraph stays where the design puts it, after the lifecycle block. The flowchart takes a new `#### EVICT execution path` heading at the end of the encode-and-evict subsection, so its declared region holds the diagram alone and both "Why…" paragraphs stay prose; the heading is additive, and no anchor is renamed. The flowchart gains one gate for the admission row, since CD-2 refuses an admission it cannot record. (6) **Arm names.** The two review-finding arms are named for their findings, MEM-CD2 and MEM-FM3 (slice 7's form), and MEM-CD2 grades rows as a delta inside the arm. (7) **HOME** reaches the predicate as `${HOME:-}` and must be absolute, so an empty HOME admits nothing | #7764 design, Changes 1–7; #7866 FM-3 and CD-2; Q17 (b) and Q13, #7684 comment 5841113925; `core/standards/design-artifact-standard.md` § 9; `stage-06-engineering.md` § 5 B3 | **APPLIED** — RED `d068b04f`, GREEN `5c30d418`, DOCS `1eec5789`; § Implementation Notes → Slice 8 |

## Implementation Notes

Authored per slice at Stage 6. Each slice names its commits, the prediction each commit stated before its check runs, and every interpretation it made of its rendered design. Check-run verdicts are read from the pull request (Q1) and recorded by the hub.

### Slice 1 — #6896 (layout helper)

**Commits** on `release/install-resolves-identically`, in order:

| Step | Commit | Carries | Prediction stated in the commit, before its check runs |
|---|---|---|---|
| B0 | `a8e5b3b5` | The frontmatter `status:` set to `ACTIVE` (row ENG-B0) | `Close-out automation smoke (macOS)` turns green; no other check changes verdict |
| RED | `04d9af7f` | The new suite `core/hooks/tests/setup-ci-layout.test.sh` (32 declared arm IDs), the two `.gitignore` entries, and the `AGENT-INVOCATION` markers around the helper's then-current documented commands (comment-only) | `Hook test harness (macOS)` and `Install/onboarding/update regression suite (macOS)` fail on the new suite: 25 of 32 arms fail and 7 pass (`LAYOUT-PRECOND` and the controls `LAYOUT-DOC-03b`, `LAYOUT-SITE-01`, `LAYOUT-DEFAULT-03`, `LAYOUT-GUARD-05`, `LAYOUT-GUARD-07`, `LAYOUT-GUARD-08`). The AC-1 arm `LAYOUT-DOC-01b` fails on the variable-bearing documented runner line. Its cause, as the RED log shows it, is the arm's own literal-line check — `the documented line is not a literal 'bash <path>.sh' command, so it cannot be admitted as written` — so the hook was not consulted; the commit's message predicted a `BLOCK-DESTRUCTIVE-022` refusal there. The hook's refusal of the variable-bearing form is proven hermetically by `LAYOUT-DOC-03a`, from GREEN on. Every other suite and check keeps its verdict |
| GREEN | `26287b92` | The helper (the default site, D-Guard, the recorded footprint and its purge, the digest, D-Mirror, USAGE), both programmatic callers, row 3, the allowlist and runner comments, the Spoke Template carve-out, and the Tier-1 [ADJUST] to `block-mcp-writes.test.sh` (row ENG-6896-ADJ) | Both macOS jobs turn green: 32 of 32 arms, the harness at `AGGREGATE: PASS=1796 FAIL=0`; every other check keeps its verdict |
| MUT | `dc6331aa` | Armed-red for AC-3: two literal allowlist rows that admit exactly the two control paths | Exactly three arms fail, in both macOS jobs: `LAYOUT-SITE-01`, `LAYOUT-DOC-02b` (its twin of the documented runner line is the same path) and `LAYOUT-DOC-03b`. The other 29 arms and every other suite pass; every other check keeps its GREEN verdict |
| REV | `43c9af7f` | The revert of MUT. Its tree equals GREEN's (`5082d5ef`); MUT's tree (`931acf7c`) differs | Both macOS jobs are green again, as at GREEN |

**Declared arm IDs (Q2 FM-5)**, the CIAC-6 set for this suite: `LAYOUT-PRECOND`; `LAYOUT-FRESH-01..04`; `LAYOUT-DOC-00`, `-01a`, `-01b`, `-02a`, `-02b`, `-03a`, `-03b`; `LAYOUT-SITE-01`; `LAYOUT-DEFAULT-01..03`; `LAYOUT-GUARD-01`, `-01b`, `-02..08`; `LAYOUT-PURGE-01..04`; `LAYOUT-FOOTPRINT-01`; `LAYOUT-IGNORE-01`; `LAYOUT-OWNER-01`. Thirty-two in all.

**The recorded footprint: the interface #5274's step 3b writes through (Q2 FM-2).**
- `layout_put copy <rel-dst> <rel-src>` copies `<repo>/<rel-src>` to `<sandbox>/<rel-dst>`.
- `layout_put stdin <rel-dst> [<rel-src>]` writes stdin there. `<rel-src>` names the tracked input the content derives from; `-` or omitted means none.
- `layout_mkdir <rel-dir>` creates a directory, recording each component it creates.
- `<rel-dst>` is relative to the sandbox root and `<rel-src>` to the repository root. Both are plain: no leading `/`, and no empty, `.` or `..` segment.
- Each call appends its record to `.claude/hooks/tests/.layout-footprint` before it writes: `f` or `d`, the destination, then the source or `-`, tab-separated.
- The purge, the build digest (`.layout-digest`) and `LAYOUT-IGNORE-01` all read that list. A file step 3b writes through `layout_put` is therefore purged on rebuild, digested, and checked for ignore coverage, with no further edit.

**Interpretations.** Where the Stage-5 design's text and the rendered decision differ, the decision governs.
1. **D-Guard (Q2 FM-3; #7860 routed item 4).**
   - The live Claude configuration directories are those of the declared workspace root (when set), of the account home's `Claude/`, and of the account home itself. The account home is read from the user database, never from `${HOME}`.
   - The guard compares filesystem identity, not path strings. It refuses (exit 65) a sandbox that is a live root, and a sandbox whose `.claude/` is, or sits inside, a live `.claude/` — except under that directory's `worktrees/<name>/`.
   - The design's `GUARD-03` graded a `${HOME}`-derived root, which Q2 retired. Two arms replace it. `LAYOUT-GUARD-03` aims at the account home with a repo root that holds no sources, so a broken guard stops at the missing sources instead of writing. `LAYOUT-GUARD-08` shows an overridden `HOME` is not a live root.
   - FM-3's "at least warn" for a primary checkout is a NOTE line at the default site.
2. **Ownership (Q2 FM-2).**
   - Ownership is the marker `.layout-owner`, written before the first copy.
   - A layout with no recorded footprint is refused with trash-then-rerun, not counted as owned. That covers every layout the pre-change helper built, whether or not it carries `.source-repo-root`, because the exact-list purge has no list to remove for it. Routing register RR-36 already names the operator housekeeping.
   - The purge validates every record before it removes anything. It removes files one at a time, and recorded directories only when empty. It refuses a record that leaves the sandbox, or that resolves outside it through a link.
3. **The design's `PURGE-01` is re-aimed.** It planted an unrecorded file and expected the purge to remove it, which an exact-list purge never does. `LAYOUT-PURGE-01` now grades the hazard the purge exists for: a suite and a hook deleted from the source since the last build. `LAYOUT-PURGE-02` grades the complement: an unrecorded file survives, and the freshness check flags it.
4. **The freshness digest (Q2 FM-1).** It holds one line per recorded file — its layout path and hash, its source path and hash — plus the helper and the manifest as build inputs. `LAYOUT-FRESH-01` reads it for the running layout. `LAYOUT-FRESH-02..04` and `LAYOUT-PURGE-02` prove each staleness limb is reported: a changed copy, a changed source, a new source, an unrecorded file. The digest's first full-harness run surfaced one suite whose restore was not byte-exact (row ENG-6896-ADJ).
5. **Ignore coverage (Q2 FM-2 (c)).** `LAYOUT-IGNORE-01` grades a default-site build's footprint, not the running layout's. An explicit sandbox also carries the readiness-corpus mirror, which a checkout-root build never writes (D-Mirror).
6. **`LAYOUT-DOC-01` fails closed** on a documented line that is not a literal `bash <path>.sh` command. Admission as written is not decidable for a line the shell must expand first.
7. **A readiness-corpus mirror left at the default site is refused** with trash-then-rerun. The doc-tie arms read `<layout>/.claude/rules/…` ahead of the corpus in place, so a stale mirror there would be graded instead of the source.
8. **V-3 was run on the pre-change tree (FM-5 (a)).** The source-tree runner read `AGGREGATE: PASS=1618 FAIL=146`, with five suites failing, so row 3 carries the source-tree sentence. **V-5 was not run:** the loading premise stays at MEDIUM (Q13), and the D-Mirror comment says "may load".
9. **Armed-red-then-revert is scoped to AC-3's arms.** AC-3 is the card criterion whose arms grade behaviour that was already correct before the fix. The other arms that pass before the fix (`LAYOUT-DEFAULT-03`, `LAYOUT-GUARD-05`, `-07`, `-08`) are controls that make their paired RED arms discriminate.
10. **FM-7 (a)'s hub-directive half is brief text, not a file** (transcription interpretation 1). The Spoke Template half is the edit in `release/references/how-to/hub-spoke-bridge.md` § Run-Directory Discipline.

**A3 — Step 0 classification of the 17.** Every arm asserts that a target spelled from the operator's home lies outside every allowed root.

| # | Arm, as the suite names it | The target the hook classifies | Expected | Class |

- **Why I.** A `mktemp -d` HOME sits in the per-user temp area. The full `AC-AP-013` name in each CI log shows the override HOME under `/var/folders/`, which normalizes to `/private/var/folders/`, and the fs-boundary allowlist admits both as scratch roots. Every target spelled from that home therefore resolves inside an allowed root, and the hook allows it. The arm's expected verdict depends on where the caller's HOME lies, which is a HOME-config dependence of the test.
- **Not R.** Step (a) is the pinned recipe, and its pin control reads exit 1 on all three arms: nothing in the job installs PyYAML, and no hook-floor arm imports it.
- **Not S.** No arm reads, writes or proves live state. The hook classifies path strings, and its normalizer does not require a path to exist.
- **Not the hook keying on `$HOME`.** The hook resolves `~` through HOME exactly as the shell that would run the command does — its registry fragment's Path Resolution, step 3 — so under an override HOME the allow is the correct verdict for the path it was given. The HOME-independent twins still block in the same run: `cat` and `xxd` of another user's home, the four EXT-POS arms, the AC-2 control and the three AC-7 G5 arms. So the hook was armed and enforcing, and the gap is the test's isolation.

**Interpretations.** Where the Stage-5 design's text and the rendered decision differ, the decision governs.
1. **The dependence is not git identity.** The decision names class I by its example, git identity. Here the dependence is where HOME lies relative to the allowlist's scratch roots, and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` does not reach the hook. What the pass carries is the pattern's shape: a fixture; a per-invocation env prefix on only the command that reads the HOME-derived input, here the hook; and a precondition arm that proves the lever live in both directions before any assertion depends on it.
2. **The fixture home is a synthetic path, not the account home.** `/nonexistent/fs-boundary-fixture-home` lies outside every allowed root under both recipes, the static roots and the HOME-derived rows alike. It is never created, because the hook's normalizer does not need the path to exist. Pinning to the account home is class S's remedy, R-8's; here it would tie the arms to the live home's filesystem, which a fixture keeps out.
3. **Exactly the 17.** The arms that assert an allow inside a HOME-derived root keep the caller's HOME. The layout helper materialized those allowlist rows from the caller's HOME, so a fixture home would move each of those arms off the row it names. Under the harness runner, which exports the master-activation and scope roots, the pinned HOME reaches the hook only through `~`. Outside the runner the master-activation read would follow the fixture home, and `HOME-FIXTURE-01` would name that failure instead of leaving 17 unexplained ones.
4. **The dd and Cowork arms** carry the home already expanded into the payload, as the shell would pass it. The fixture home is spelled into those two payloads; the other 15 arms keep their payload text byte for byte.
5. **One precondition arm is added,** the pattern's P-0. Under the fixture home a `~` target blocks and the message names the fixture path; under a home inside an allowed root, `/tmp/fs-boundary-fixture-home`, the same payload is allowed. The file goes from 81 arms to 82. No existing arm's name, expected exit or pattern changes: 71 of 71 `test_case` and `test_warn_case` calls keep their expectation, 17 carry the fixture argument, and those 17 equal the baseline's names on both SHAs.
6. **The Cowork arm keeps its verdict, not all of its reach.** Under the fixture home no allowlist row names the install path, so the arm pins that the unquoted form blocks; it no longer tells the tokenization apart from an unlisted home. A comment at the arm says so.
7. **The Contention "row".** The table in § Contention lists files that two or more cards edit, so this file is recorded by the single-editor sentence after it, the form the plan already uses for a file one card owns.

**A2 — the baseline, read from CI.** The head's own run had not finished at entry, so the baseline is `60bd32b0`'s run `36359793657`. Its tree differs from the head's only in this file.
- **Install/onboarding/update regression suite** (job `108734550909`), its four steps:

**A3 — the anchors at the head, bound by text.**
- `docs/scripts/setup-workspace.sh`, `docs/INSTALL.md`, `core/deploy/tests/test_refresh_hooks.sh` and `install.sh` carry no change since the design pin `8e0ee084` (an empty diff; control: the durability member shows 257 changed lines). So the design's installer anchors stand: five human-answer reads (`:1078`, `:2440`, `:2870`, `:3543`, `:4467`), `NON_INTERACTIVE` consulted at three sites (`:1045`, `:1131`, `:4385`), and each Change-1 anchor occurring exactly once.
- P-10's census, the amended body below, run statically over the head's installer: `5 1 2440,2870,3543,4467`. The plan's control string holds at the head.
- DEV-9's joined-line census over the durability member: 14 `"${SETUP}"` lines, subject 0, control 10.
- The member's insertion points, both outside slice 2's regions: the header's Suite P paragraph (`:43-44` at entry) and the line before `# --- Stage 5c (Suite C)` (`:984` at entry).

**Interpretations.** Where the Stage-5 design's text and the rendered decision differ, the decision governs.
1. **The CI step is the job's last, not beside the plain step.** A step's default condition skips it once an earlier step fails. Beside the plain step, each red run of the new step (B0 and RED, by design) would have skipped every later Shell harness step, the gate-efficacy ratchet included. Last, under `!cancelled()`, it skips nothing and is never skipped. That is slice 2's precedent for an observer leg. Row ENG-4994-PA004 records it.
2. **The CI step's program** is the design's eight lines plus the sentinel line PA-004 names. The design's program kills the group and exits 124 without one. The prompt Case 12 is held at is captured inside the case (`OUT12`), so the log cannot show it. The record of "held in Case 12" is therefore the last case header before the sentinel, with no `12-mode` line after it.
3. **The job's rules for newly wired steps.** The timeout guard keys on a suite named on a step's `run:` line. Listing `test_refresh_hooks.sh` there would have made the plain step, which carries no timeout, an owning step, and would have reddened the probe; the new step names the suite on a later line. So the guard finds the new step by its name. The probe's second arm breaks a suite at `[ "${FAIL}" -ne 0 ] && exit 1`, which `test_refresh_hooks.sh` does not carry. What the new step adds is the harness, so the fourth arm breaks that instead. It reads the program out of the step itself and requires three outcomes: a stub that reads stdin is held and exits 124 with the sentinel; `exit 3` passes through; `exit 0` passes through with no sentinel.
4. **P-10, as Q6 (a) renders it.** The fixture's three must-flag lines could not be flagged by the design's predicate, so the census learned three rules as well as the redirect rule:
   - a `while`/`until` read loop is data only when a pipe leads into it, or when its condition or its `done` carries an input redirection (`done` matched at the loop's own indentation);
   - a `builtin` or `command` prefix still marks a read;
   - a guard counts only when it is an `if [ "${NON_INTERACTIVE}" -eq 1 ]` block at no deeper indentation than the read, closed before it, with no `&&` and no else or elif, whose block ends in `return` or `exit`.

   Its comment now states that boundary as its whole claim (#7861 PR-1). Validated statically:
   - the fixture reads `6 1 8,11,26,29,35`, where the design's predicate reads `5 2 8,11,38` on the same fixture, reproducing PR-1's misses and FM-1's false positive;
   - both predicates read `3 1 8,11` on the design's own fixture;
   - the head installer reads `5 1 2440,2870,3543,4467`, and the patched one `5 5 -`;
   - the patched installer plus a #5265-shaped heredoc-fed `read` reads `5 5 -`, where the design's predicate reads `6 5 <line>`.
5. **The redirect rule has one exception:** a redirection from the terminal (`/dev/tty`, `/dev/stdin`, `/dev/fd/0`, `<&0`) still reads a human answer, so it does not make a read a data read. Without the exception the rule would open the false-negative class it exists to close. It fails closed.
6. **FM-2's per-arm markers.**
   - The harness writes a START marker. Each of P-9c, P-9e and P-9f requires it in its own log.
   - The design's P-9b ("no `OPEN-STDIN-HARNESS` line") would then have failed on every run, so P-9b is keyed on the TIMEOUT sentinel and requires the START marker.
   - The P-9e precondition requires the drift branch's own `DRIFT:` line, which prints before the prompt, guarded or not. P-9e's pass requires the `PRESERVED (non-interactive)` line.
   - Every install arm's failure reports the harness start, the sentinel and its own-prompt line count, so a RED reads "held at its own prompt".
   - The TIMEOUT sentinel is matched unanchored: a held prompt leaves a partial line, and the sentinel can land after it.
7. **P-9f grades the schema-mismatch trigger.** AC-1's row has P-9f's recovery "naming the schema-mismatch trigger" (Q14). So the E66 refusal names it, and P-9f requires a fixed substring of that line (`schema_version is missing or is not`).
8. **The corrected downstream contract (Q6).** The design told #5265 to answer a flagged `read` with a flag-only branch, which would leave its variables empty under the flag. The corrected statement lives in P-10's comment, where a flagged read leads: a read fed by its own redirection is a data read, is not counted, and must never be wrapped in a `NON_INTERACTIVE` branch. #5265's heredoc-fed split passes the census as it stands, and #5265 never records an empty root (Q6).
9. **Arms born green, and the one pair that does not discriminate.**
   - The P-9 precondition, the P-9e precondition and P-10's fixture control are fixture proofs.
   - P-9a and P-9b are opposite outcomes of one harness, so each makes the other falsifiable.
   - P-9g is not discriminated by the RED/GREEN pair: it passes on both trees. It grades behaviour that was already correct before the fix: a flagless piped install is still prompted.
   - A one-line MUT was prepared, a `|| [ ! -t 0 ]` disjunct on hook activation's new guard, which would have failed P-9g alone. It was not committed. The session's auto-mode classifier refused the verification of the weakened guard as a security weakening, and the spoke did not route around the refusal. The worktree was restored to GREEN's tree. P-9g's sensitivity therefore rests on its construction (it greps Stage 1's flagless, piped install log for the activation prompt, which a stdin-keyed guard would silence) and is unobserved. That is routed to the hub. A committed armed pair exists since the Stage 7 batched fix cycle: `cf5e544f` arms that mutant and `de931cbd` reverts it byte for byte, under the operator's decision PA-006; its red is graded in the hub's CI record on #7783.
10. **No case other than Case 12 is expected to block under the held-open stdin.** By static read:
    - Cases 1-11 and 13-16 run `--refresh-hooks`, `--restore-hooks` or `--reconcile-hooks`. The refresh branch returns before the drift prompt and never calls hook activation, and `--reconcile-hooks` sets the refresh flag.
    - Case 11b's background refresh gets `/dev/null` for stdin.
    - Case 17 reads `update.sh` with `awk`.
    - The file has no shell `read`, and every `python3` program in it arrives on a heredoc.
    - In the installer, every JSON helper and every `jq` call takes a file, and the only Python stdin reads are pipe-fed.

    Case 12's flagged re-bootstrap reaches hook activation because its config root carries no `master_enabled`. Its token prompts are guarded, and its hooks match source.

**Interpretations.** Where the Stage-5 design's text and the rendered decision differ, the decision governs.
1. **The composed block's blank lines.** §3–§7 and §9 go in above Arm 6's banner. The existing blank line after Arm 5 now precedes §3, which carries none of its own, and one blank line stays directly above the banner. That reproduces the merged text's own composed count, 1333 lines.
2. **Change 11 is not fired** (row ND-6). The composition-lib step runs ahead of the compose-units step in the Shell harness job, so a RED arm there would have hidden `test_compose.py`'s RED. **The coverage boundary this leaves, stated so it is not over-read:** in every fixture arm the recorded root and the main working tree coincide, so the fixture cannot tell a root threaded through the lib from a tier-5 fallback inside `compose.py`. Direct evidence of the threading rests on reading Changes 2–4.
3. **The documents land in their own commit** (row ENG-5265-READINGS). The design's "one commit" names the four code files, and partial landing is unsafe only between them.
4. **Identity, not membership (#7862 PR-2).** A tree is a checkout only when it is its work tree's top level, tested by filesystem identity. A tree nested inside another work tree — a platform checkout or an unrelated repository — resolves to itself as a declared source, subject to the marker check.
5. **The provenance rule (Q15 (b), CD-2).** A record carrying `source_repo_path_source` is honored after validation. A legacy record is validated the same way, then compared with tier 5: agreeing, it is used with a NOTE; with tier 5 unresolvable, it is used with a NOTE naming why; disagreeing, one WARN line names both roots and `--force-regen`, and tier 5 wins. The WARN is a single line starting `WARN:`, which Arm 9b's check reads, and a skipped record reads `NOTE: install-record tier skipped:`, which Arm 9 leaves in `9-w1.err` for slice 5's Arm 11.
6. **The empty-record guard (Q6).** Without a composition library the installer records the raw `--source-repo` value with a WARN and omits the provenance key, so the next update treats it as advisory. A root that cannot be resolved stops the install with exit 66 before any install step.
7. **The resolver's git calls run with stdin closed.** The P-9 arms and Case 12 run installs with stdin held open; git reads none, and the resolver never offers it any.
8. **Restatements (#7862 PR-3).** RK1's trigger — git cannot interrogate a git-clone checkout, whatever the record says — and AT-2's scope — `update.sh` refuses the whole run in pre-flight — are stated in the spec's § 2 and in the Consequences of {{ADR:pmo-platform-root-resolves-from-install-record}}.
9. **Two documentation clauses wait on slice 5.** The spec's "reports the value and the tier that supplied it" (INT-2's subject) and `docs/UPDATE.md` § 3.2's "prints the value and where it came from" become true with slice 5's report line. This slice prints NOTE and WARN lines only.
10. **RR-14 is not folded in.** `source_repo_sha` stays empty for an install from a linked worktree; no decision rendered the optional fix, the value has no reader, and PR-2 showed the membership-based fix proposed for it is wrong.
11. **A resolver spike, not a suite run.** Before GREEN, the new `compose.py` resolver was exercised on scratch git repositories created and deleted inside the spoke's run directory: 35 of 35 intended checks passed, and a deliberately wrong control failed. It is evidence for the resolver's logic only; the graded record is CI's. No install, update, deploy or composition suite ran on the operator's machine (AI-015).
12. **The record's number and its citations (Q3).** The filename carries the literal number, claimed at authorship against the mainline anchor. This plan's prose cites the record by its slug token, which the Stage-12 stamp resolves; the detector's own output is quoted literally on a row marked as a record, so a renumber leaves that quote true.

**Interpretations.** Where the Stage-5 design's text and the rendered decision differ, the decision governs. Row ENG-5274-READINGS records the readings; these notes add what grades them.
1. **E2E-6 first, and what it exercises.** Its five lines landed first among the new arms. The deployed shape runs the hook and the writer with `CLAUDE_WORKSPACE_ROOT`, `PMO_INSTANCE_PATH` and `PMO_SCOPE_GUARD_ROOT` unset, the location branch production takes. The mutation arm counts the hook's two-level derivation (it must occur exactly once), cuts one level and must block. The layout arm reads the running layout's own list, which only step 3b writes.
2. **INT-3, as landed.** Step 3b records the copy as one footprint entry with its tracked source. The exact-list purge removes it on rebuild like every recorded file, and the digest covers it. E2E-6's layout arm grades the read, and `LAYOUT-FOOTPRINT-01` and `LAYOUT-IGNORE-01` grade the default site's footprint and ignore coverage. No arm removes this file's source to watch the purge take it; `LAYOUT-PURGE-01` grades that mechanism on other recorded files.
3. **AC-1's scope (PR-1).** One path holds on the default root, or on a root exported as `CLAUDE_WORKSPACE_ROOT`. The two residuals — the deploy-side readers' ambient root, and a `PMO_INSTANCE_PATH` split between the install shell and the Claude Code process — are recorded in the resolver's header and in the ADR, and routed to the hook-root follow-up.
4. **The reconcile's gate on its reader.** The phase compares the deployed Gate 2 hook with its source byte for byte and requires the co-deployed resolver. That comparison asks one question of one hook. `refresh_hooks` keeps asserting on its delegate's exit code alone, so Case 17's check on that body holds.
5. **A residual beside step 3b, not introduced by it.** In a layout this helper owns, `layout_put` overwrites an existing regular file that is absent from the footprint. That holds for every layout path; step 3b adds one path outside `.claude/`. The unowned-layout refusal covers the new path, and the identity guard (R-8) keeps a live workspace out of reach.
6. **The repair's verification.** The defective construct, run under the system bash 3.2, reproduces "syntax error near unexpected token" (the sensitivity arm). The repaired shape is the shape of `e2e_row_target`, which passed E2E-1 in every local harness run of this slice under that same bash. A parse check is not a detector for this class. The graded record of the repair is the install-regression job on `a8d12e3d`.
7. **Local runs.** Only the hook harness ran locally, through the Q2 carve-out at this spoke's default site with `PMO_REGRESSION_EMIT=0`. It read `AGGREGATE: PASS=1801 FAIL=7` on C2's tree, `PASS=1807 FAIL=1` on C3's and `PASS=1808 FAIL=0` on C5c's, each as predicted. No install, update, deploy or composition suite ran on the operator's machine, under any HOME (AI-015).

3. **The empty-root guard is defense in depth, and no arm pins it.** Both views resolve through the same `script_realpath`, so today a root that fails to resolve while the target resolves is not reachable; an arm could only reach it through a mutated resolver.

- **The fragment:** its Scope row at `:8`, its -001 and -003 rows at `:15` and `:17`, the Trash-command auto-detection list at `:36-40` and the closing "See …" line at `:42`.
- **§ 7:** the lifecycle block closes at `:298`, and "Why encode-then-evict is mandatory" follows at `:300`. The ARCHIVE text #5185 owns sits inside the block, so it is disjoint from the insertion.
- **Item 8** sits at `:189-191`.
- **The ADR detector:** anchor 206 on `origin/main`, with the mainline unmoved since the branch base; next-free 207; this branch holds 207 and 208, so the record takes 209.

**B0 — Step 0 classification of the 7.** Each arm asserts inside-workspace behaviour on a target spelled from the caller's HOME.

| # | Arm, as the suite names it | The target the hook classifies | Expected | Class |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `rm inside Claude blocks with Trash suggestion` | `foo.txt`, relative to the default working directory under the caller's HOME | exit 2, `-002` | I |
| 2 | `rm absolute inside Claude blocks-trash` | the home, expanded by the test, then `/Claude/.claude/worktrees/foo/bar.txt` | exit 2, `-002` | I |
| 3 | `rmdir inside Claude blocks-trash` | the home, expanded by the test, then `/Claude/tmp/empty` | exit 2, `-002` | I |
| 4 | `trash inside Claude allows` | the home, expanded by the test, then `/Claude/foo.txt` | exit 0 | I |
| 5 | `osascript Trash-verb inside allows` | the home, expanded by the test, then `/Claude/foo`, in the POSIX file | exit 0 | I |
| 6 | `AC-AP-012: /usr/bin/rm foo.txt (worktree cwd) blocks-trash (BLOCK-TRASH-002)` | `foo.txt`, relative to the default working directory | exit 2, `-002` | I |
| 7 | `AC-AP-013: /bin/unlink <home>/Claude/foo blocks-trash (BLOCK-TRASH-002)` | the home, expanded by the test, then `/Claude/foo` | exit 2, `-002` | I |

- **Why I.** The hook spells its default workspace root from HOME as written and canonicalizes the target. So each arm's expected verdict holds only while the caller's HOME is a canonical path. The override HOME is a `mktemp -d` directory under `/var/folders/`, reached through the `/var` → `/private/var` symlink. The target resolves to the `/private` form, the root keeps the `/var` form, and each inside target reads as outside: exit 2 where 0 was expected, or the -001 or -003 rule where -002 was. That is a HOME-config dependence of the test.
- **Not R.** Step (a) is the pinned recipe, and its pin control reads exit 1 on all three arms. The hook's python3 is the system interpreter with the standard library only.
- **Not S.** No arm reads, writes or proves live state. The hook classifies path strings and does not need them to exist.
- **The hook's comparison** of a canonicalized target with a root spelled as written is its own behaviour. The design recorded it and routed it out of this card. PA-003 is test isolation only, so the hook is unchanged and the arms pin a canonical fixture home.
- **Evidence.** The CI log forwards each arm's FAIL line, not its stderr. The classification therefore rests on the code path and on the in-harness precondition arm, the evidence slice 2's second pass used.

**B0's interpretations.**
1. **The fixture** is `/nonexistent/rm-trash-fixture-home`, a synthetic path that is never created — slice 2's second-pass shape. The property this suite needs is canonicality: nothing in the path can be a symlink, so the root the hook spells and the target it resolves agree.
2. **The lever** is `test_case`'s existing fifth argument, so no helper changes. The five payloads that spelled the caller's HOME are spelled from the fixture, and the two default-cwd arms pass the fixture's working directory explicitly.
3. **AC-AP-013's name** still expands the caller's HOME: its source line is byte-identical, so its arm ID reads as it always has. Its payload uses the fixture, and a comment says so.
4. **`HOME-FIXTURE-01`** observes both directions. Under the fixture HOME, an rm of a target in the fixture's workspace is redirected (BLOCK-TRASH-002) naming the fixture path. Under a second synthetic HOME, the same payload is refused as outside (BLOCK-TRASH-001). Its failure message names `CLAUDE_WORKSPACE_ROOT`, which the hook prefers to HOME.
5. **The counts.** There are 53 `test_case` calls and 54 arms before and after the change. Every name, expected exit and pattern is unchanged, and exactly the 7 baseline names carry the fixture argument. The added arm makes 55.

**The RED run, against the unchanged hook** (local, Q2 carve-out): `block-rm-prefer-trash.test.sh — Total: 85  PASS: 75  FAIL: 10`, exactly the predicted ten, and `AGGREGATE: PASS=1867 FAIL=10`.
- MEM-01..MEM-05, MEM-27b, MEM-CD2's admission limb and MEM-FM3 are refused by BLOCK-TRASH-003's generic text, "deletion outside Claude/ is forbidden — cancel operation". That is AC-1's "RED before".
- MEM-06 and MEM-07 are refused by BLOCK-TRASH-001's generic text, where the memory-specific message is expected.
- Every other MEM arm passes before the fix as a regression lock. CI on `d068b04f` read the same ten arms.

**The GREEN run:** `Total: 85  PASS: 85  FAIL: 0`, `AGGREGATE: PASS=1877 FAIL=0`, with `setup-ci-layout.test.sh` at 32/32 and `block-destructive.test.sh` at 672/672.

**The arms this slice adds** — 31, so the file goes from 54 to 85:
- **B0:** `HOME-FIXTURE-01`.
- **RED, the design's 28:** MEM-01..MEM-26, MEM-27a and MEM-27b.
- **RED, the review's 2:** MEM-CD2 (#7866 CD-2) and MEM-FM3 (#7866 FM-3).

MEM-10, MEM-18, MEM-CD2 and MEM-FM3 each carry more than one payload and pass only when every one holds:
- **MEM-10:** the index as written, and in four forms the shell would expand or unescape into it, each also a literal file name in the store.
- **MEM-18:** a symlink in the store that escapes it, and a symlink outside it that resolves in.
- **MEM-CD2:** (a) an admission adds exactly one row carrying the command's digest and the cwd and no `rule`; (b) a command refused as a whole adds none; (c1) a sandbox copy admits while its log is writable; (c2) the same copy refuses once a row cannot be written.
- **MEM-FM3:** (i) project scope, through the project root passed to hooks; (ii) local scope, through the working directory.

**Mutation evidence** — one combined mutant of four disjoint mutations, each caught by its own arm. The mutant ran with the layout rebuilt from it; the source stayed mutated through the run, so `LAYOUT-FRESH-01` passed.

| Mutation | Change | Expected | Observed |
|---|---|---|---|
| M-1 | the operand's character rule accepts any character | MEM-10 fails | MEM-10 FAIL: its four expansion forms admitted; the index as written still refused |
| M-2 | the drafted predicate's whole-operand resolution, with no symlink-leaf check | MEM-18 fails | MEM-18 FAIL: the outside link admitted; the escaping link still refused |
| M-3 | a failed row write no longer refuses | MEM-CD2 fails on (c2) | MEM-CD2 FAIL on (c2) alone |
| M-4 | project and local scope admit | MEM-FM3 fails | MEM-FM3 FAIL on (i) and (ii) |

The run read `Total: 85  PASS: 81  FAIL: 4`, exactly those four arms, with `AGGREGATE: PASS=1873 FAIL=4`. The source was then restored byte-identical to GREEN's and the layout rebuilt, and the final GREEN run read 85/85, `AGGREGATE: PASS=1877 FAIL=0`.

**The documents.**
- **§ 7 is the mechanism's single home.** It gains one paragraph after the lifecycle block, and the Tier-A flowchart under a new `#### EVICT execution path` heading with its design-artifact marker. No heading or anchor is renamed, and #5185's ARCHIVE region is untouched (R16).
- **Item 8** points to 8a, and its "No bypass mechanism" becomes "No agent-reachable bypass": `CLAUDE_HOOK_BYPASS` is set before launch, and a mid-session assignment is refused by BLOCK-DESTRUCTIVE-023, which the hook and its registry row both state. **Item 8a** follows 6a's and 7a's shape.
- **The ADR** mirrors {{ADR:exemption-list-resolves-at-the-instance-tier-through-one-resolver}}'s shape. Its issue references sit only in its References block.
- **AC-3's census:** UNCITED reads exactly the baseline's three N/A blocks. The new blocks in `autonomy-tiers.md`, the fragment and the index read CITED, and § 7 holds 8 HOME blocks.

**Interpretations.** Row ENG-6440-READINGS records the readings of the rendered design. These notes add what grades them:
1. **Operand first** is graded by construction. No pre-existing arm of the suite names a `.md` operand under the caller's HOME, so no arm opens the caller's settings file. The probe found 0 of 47 such arms, and a planted `.md` arm was flagged by the same extraction.
2. **The character rule and the symlink-leaf rule** are graded by M-1 and M-2 through MEM-10 and MEM-18. Without MEM-10's decoys, the existence check would also refuse the expansion forms, and the character rule would go ungraded.
3. **CD-2's conditional admission** is graded by M-3 through MEM-CD2 (c2). Its row-per-admission count is graded by (a) and (b).
4. **FM-3 never admits** — graded by M-4 through MEM-FM3.
5. **The residual stays named and out of scope:** eviction has no read-back of its own.

**Local runs.** Only the hook harness ran locally, through the Q2 carve-out at this spoke's default site with `PMO_REGRESSION_EMIT=0` on every run: the B0 run, the RED run, the GREEN runs and the mutant run. To read the RED and mutant arms' stderr, which the runner discards, the suite was also run alone at the same site with the scope root set as the runner sets it. No install, update, deploy or composition suite ran on the operator's machine, under any HOME (AI-015), and no `test-run` event was emitted.
